- CI only, downloaded by GitHub-hosted runners once the user enables msil-ci.yml: actions/setup-dotnet (tag v4.x, SHA to be pinned by the user), NuGet runtime.linux-x64.Microsoft.NETCore.ILAsm 10.0.12 and runtime.linux-x64.Microsoft.NETCore.ILDAsm 10.0.12, dotnet tool dotnet-ilverify 10.0.12, apt packages ninja-build clang lld, choco package ninja, and sccache via hendrikmuhs/ccache-action@f09c25b45002a07be2955cbe52e8cee55643f89d (v1.2.24, same SHA as upstream).

## 9. Risks the plan tracks

- **JIT hangs on bad IL.** The .NET 10 JIT hung and allocated 40+ GB on an inconsistent stack depth at a join
  (cil-runtime e15). Every dotnet/jitall run is under a timeout and a memory cap (M0); MSILStackCheck makes such IL a
  compile-time error.
- **Silent type errors.** The JIT accepts stack-type errors (float64 into an int32 local, int64 returned as int32,
  int64/native int mixing). The single-class-per-CIL-type design (D2), PEVerify on Windows, verification enforced in
  every gate (D38) and execution against a native oracle are the defences.
- **Runtime differences.** `conv.r.un`, out-of-range float-to-int, the alignment of constant-size localloc, and Tier-0
  versus optimized code differ between .NET 8, 9, 10 and Framework. FP, frame and memory gates run on .NET 8 (tiered
  and TieredCompilation=0), .NET 10 and Framework (M5, M7, M10).
- **Capabilities needed before their milestone.** Generic SelectionDAG legalization and the IR optimizer reach
  features early: SELECT from i128 shifts and min/max, BITCAST through memory from fabs/copysign and the generic
  u64->f64 expansion, lookup-table globals from switches, TTI queries at -O2, `@__const` globals from initialized
  local aggregates, frames from every clang -O0 function. Defences: SELECT in M4 (D34), the memory-free D17 sequence,
  lookup tables off (D8), query hooks final in M2 (D33) and the capability table of section 5.5, which the
  test-adequacy reviewer checks in every milestone.
- **Crashes after diagnostics.** llc and clang keep running after an error. MSILPrepareIR neutralizes what it
  diagnoses, LowerCall builds no call for a diagnosed callee, and every diagnostic test rejects `LLVM ERROR` output
  (section 2).
- **Stub drift.** Stubs fail loudly with their milestone; the scope check stops WPs from editing other WPs' files.
- **Upstream merge cost.** Upstream edits are confined to the M1 hooks; copied WebAssembly files carry their source
  commit (D30).
- **Machine contention.** Builds throttle on low RAM; background builds die with the agent's turn, so the build agent
  waits for its build. D: has ~34 GB free; no second build tree there.
- **Shared working tree.** Parallel WPs see each other's files mid-edit. Dependencies only gate checks (1.2a), owners
  of shared headers write the contract declarations first, and the build is serialized; per-agent worktrees are ruled
  out by disk space and build time.
- **Lost inputs.** Everything a WP reads is in the repository (`recon/`, `recon/artifacts/`); `plan-check.py reads`
  runs before every milestone.
- **CRLF checkout.** `msil/.gitattributes` keeps expected files LF regardless of `core.autocrlf=true`.
- **Undefined functions.** With `-ffreestanding` the optimizer introduces no libc calls. An undefined function that is
  not a Phase-1 import is a compile-time error (D14), so a missing helper never turns into a runtime
  EntryPointNotFoundException; compiler-rt helpers are diagnosed until phase 3.

## 10. Decisions that need the user

1. Verification criterion (Design.md section 12, 'ilverify reports no stack-shape errors'): ILVerify stops at the first error per method and cannot verify any method containing calli, so the literal criterion is not checkable on real C code. Proposed restatement (decision D21): MSILStackCheck (always on) + jitall force-JIT under time/memory limits + ILVerify with a code allow-list + PEVerify on Windows (allowing exactly the native-int arguments of Buffer::MemoryCopy), all clean and enforced in every gate. Approve, or specify another criterion. Agents will not edit Design.md.
   **Resolved 2026-10-12: user approved D21 (combined MSILStackCheck + jitall + ILVerify allow-list + PEVerify), enforced in every gate. Refinement for the D12 RVA image copy and ldftn-as-native-int PEVerify reports (section 12, M0) confirmed by the user the same day.**
2. Frame model (Design.md 4.2): keep one localloc per frame in Phase 1 (implemented in M7 behind a single helper), or switch now to an address-taken explicit-layout value-type local, which RyuJIT compiles to a 4-byte prologue versus 73 bytes (GS cookie, rbp frame, probe) for localloc (passes-frame recon). Recommendation: localloc now, measure in phase 4.
   **Resolved 2026-10-12: localloc per frame in Phase 1 behind one helper; measure in phase 4 (D13 stands).**
3. Phase-1 assembly references (decision D10): emit only '.assembly extern mscorlib' (runs unchanged on .NET 8/9/10 and .NET Framework 4.8, verified) and no DisableRuntimeMarshalling (not needed for blittable preservesig signatures), deferring Design.md 5.3/7.1's System.Runtime + DisableRuntimeMarshalling header to the phase-3 -mdotnet profile. Confirm.
   **Resolved 2026-10-12: mscorlib only in Phase 1 (D10 stands); ~~System.Runtime header with the phase-3 -mdotnet profile~~. Revised 2026-10-14 (section 12): there is no -mdotnet profile, no core-library switch and no target-framework option; from P2-B on msil-ld takes its BCL assembly references from the reference assemblies it is given (-r), as Roslyn does, and DisableRuntimeMarshalling is an individual codegen switch (P3-A).**
4. Phase-1 triple scope in clang (decision D6): x86_64/aarch64 on Linux (gnu, musl) and Windows (msvc, gnu) only; aarch64-apple-darwin-msil and i686-pc-windows-msvc-msil are rejected until phases 4/5 (they need the UserLabelPrefix rework for m:o/m:x layouts). Confirm.
   **Resolved 2026-10-12: D6 default accepted (darwin and i686 MSIL triples rejected until phases 4/5).**
5. GitHub/CI (branch names updated 2026-10-13: the integration branch is `msil`; `main` stays a pure upstream mirror for downsyncs): which branch becomes the fork's default (msil or msil-milestone1); whether the repository is public (hosted-runner size and cost; a cold build is 1.5-3 h); whether to enable msil-ci.yml during Phase 1; and running .github/msil/disable-upstream-workflows.sh (a repository-settings change only you should make).
   **Resolved 2026-10-12: M0.e commits msil-ci.yml with workflow_dispatch only (no push/PR triggers). Default branch, repository visibility, enabling triggers and running disable-upstream-workflows.sh remain user actions.**
6. This checkout has core.autocrlf=true (CRLF working tree). msil/.gitattributes forces LF under msil/ either way; switching the clone to core.autocrlf=false and renormalizing is your call (upstream recommends false on Windows).
   **Default 2026-10-12: leave core.autocrlf as is; msil/.gitattributes forces LF under msil/.**
7. Security (user action, not a design decision): the cil-runtime recon found plaintext GitHub feed credentials (PATs) in %APPDATA%/NuGet/NuGet.Config. Consider rotating them or moving them to a credential provider. Agents are instructed never to read or copy that file.
8. ~~Optional download: the .NET 8 runtime inside WSL Ubuntu.~~ Resolved 2026-10-12: installs are pre-approved and .NET 8.0.31 is installed in WSL (section 8).
9. __ELF__ on Linux MSIL triples: clang defines __ELF__ only when the object format is ELF (clang/lib/Frontend/InitPreprocessor.cpp), so x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu-msil does not define it, a deviation from Design.md section 3's 'predefines are exactly the native ones'. Default for Phase 1: leave it undefined (M1.f pins this with an explicit test; freestanding tests do not depend on it). Alternative: define it in the MSILTargetInfo wrapper for Linux triples so glibc headers see what they see natively. Choose.
   **Default 2026-10-12: leave __ELF__ undefined in Phase 1 (M1.f pins it); revisit with host-libc headers in phase 3.**

## 11. Notes for the orchestrator

- The planning run saved `llvm/docs/MSIL/recon/cil-runtime.md` and `build-env.md` as indexes reconstructed from the
  recon experiments, and copied the experiment sources, logs, the tests-ci prototype and the review experiments to
  `llvm/docs/MSIL/recon/artifacts/`. The orchestrator appended the original report texts from the workflow journal
  (done 2026-10-12).
- The harness sometimes refuses subagent writes of `.md` files ("return findings as text"). A WP that owns a markdown
  file (for example M10.d) returns the content in its result and the orchestrator writes it, or writes it from Bash.
- Until M0 lands, use `D:/llvm-msil/build/msil-scripts/*.ps1` (same arguments). The build directory is configured but
  not built; the first build is M1.0's TableGen pre-build, the first full build is M1's gate.
- Gate commands that build must run with `run_in_background` and be waited for; a background job dies with the turn.
- Before spawning a milestone (M1 on): `plan-check.py ownership` and `plan-check.py reads M<n>`. During the fix loop,
  assign unowned files only through section 1.7a.
- Spawn every WP of a milestone at once. Dependent WPs wait only for their checks and hand-over (1.2a). M2's check
  order: M2.b and M2.e; then M2.f and M2.c; then M2.a and M2.d.
- The structured plan returned with this document is generated from the same source as this file (scratch
  `recon/plan-revise/plan_rev.py` + `render_plan.py`).

(1) `Cannot modify an imaged based (RVA) static`, in pass 1 only, and only when the ildasm listing shows `ldsflda valuetype '<msil>image_t' '<msil>image'` at the reported offset (the image copy of section 5.1 and M8.c: `ldsfld native int '<msil>data'; ldsflda ...; ldc.i4 N; cpblk`); pass 2 rewrites that ldsflda to `ldc.i4.0; conv.i`. (2) An ldftn value used as a native int (D2; FUNC_ADDR prints `ldftn <member-ref>`), which PEVerify types as a method pointer and then rejects with a message that depends on the use (`[found <type>::<method>][expected Native Int] Unexpected type on the stack.` as an argument, local or return value; `[found <type>::<method>][expected Native Int] Non-compatible types on the stack.` or `[found Native Int] Non-compatible types on the stack.` in a compare, depending on the operand order; `[found <type>::<method>] Expected numeric type on the stack.` in arithmetic): in pass 1, PEVerify errors in a method that contains ldftn are notes, but only when pass 2 ran; pass 2 rewrites every `ldftn <sig>` to `ldc.i4.0; conv.i` and verifies that method again. Pass 2 keeps no PEVerify allowance except MemoryCopy's, so D21's 'every other Unexpected type on the stack stays fatal' still holds for the neutralized copy, which is what catches errors behind these constructs; without ildasm (no pass 2) neither allowance applies | review-r2 findings 1 and 2, reproduced by the verifiers (scratch `verify-d12-rva`, `verify-fnptr`) and by triage (`triage-r2/rev.il`: compare with the ldftn value second, ldftn plus an offset): PEVerify reports these on every module with an initialized global or a function pointer used as a value, so with `msil_require_verify=1` the M8, M9 and M10 gates could not pass on Windows; no emitted shape avoids them (`ldftn; conv.i` gives 'Expected numeric type on the stack') |

| 2026-10-13 | M4 | Plan 5.4 'TrapsOnlyOnUB' clarified (DIV_S, DIV_U, REM_S and REM_U on I32 and I64): the flag lets MSILRegStackify move a division or remainder past a call or another division, because it throws only on undefined behaviour, but it never lets a call move below a division or remainder; and instruction selection keeps a division or remainder after every call and llvm.trap that precedes it in its block, at every optimization level. A call that does not return (it traps, or later exits or loops) means the division is never executed, so running it first adds a trap the program does not have. M4.b keeps that ISel order: the i32/i64 sdiv, udiv, srem and urem SDNodes have no chain, so the -O1+ Sched::RegPressure list scheduler may place them above a CALL or TRAP. M4 fix-2 makes MSILRegStackify::isSafeToMove count an intervening TrapsOnlyOnUB instruction as an effect when the instruction it moves is a call. Later owners of MSILISelLowering.cpp (M5.a, M6.a, M7.b, M8.a) keep the order | M4 review-r2 finding 2 (verified with the gate-built llc on x86_64-pc-windows-msvc-msil: in `%c = call i32 @f(i32 %x)` (f traps), `%d = sdiv i32 %x, %y`, `add i32 %d, %c`, the div prints before the call at -O1, -O2 and -O3, so g(1, 0) dies with DivideByZeroException instead of f's NullReferenceException; -O0 is correct). It is reachable from C at -O2 (a validator call before x / y), and llvm.trap before a division behaves the same way. Both mechanisms reproduce: the order right after finalize-isel, and MSILRegStackify sinking the call below the div with -pre-RA-sched=source |
| 2026-10-13 | M4 | fix-loop ownership: `llvm/lib/Target/MSIL/MSILRegStackify.cpp` -> fix-2 | M4 review-r2 finding 2 (row 'Plan 5.4 TrapsOnlyOnUB clarified' above): query() gives a TrapsOnlyOnUB division no effects, so isSafeToMove lets a CALL that is stackified into a later use sink below an intervening DIV (verified: with -pre-RA-sched=source the MIR order CALL, DIV, ADD becomes DIV, CALL, ADD after msil-reg-stackify). fix-2 counts an intervening TrapsOnlyOnUB instruction as an effect when the moved instruction is a call, and still lets the division itself sink. MSILRegStackify.cpp has no owner after M3 |

| 2026-10-13 | M5 | M5.a extended (int64 to float32 under JIT constant folding): ISD::SINT_TO_FP from i64 to f32 (strict forms included) no longer selects a bare conv.r4. The lowering first rounds the native int to odd at bit 11 when its magnitude needs more than 53 bits, with no SELECT, branch or memory (verified form: t = x xor (x >>s 63); f = ((t >>u 53) + 1023) >>u 10; m = f * 0x7FF; x' = (x and not m) or (((x and m) + m) and (m + 1))), then emits conv.r4 of x'. x' is exact in float64 and rounds to the same float32 as x, so the result is correctly rounded both when conv.r4 executes and when the .NET Framework 4.8 JIT constant-folds conv.r4 of an int64 as (float32)(float64)x. The sint_to_fp to f32 inside LowerUINT_TO_FP (D17) gets the same step. float64 results, int32 sources and the other conversions are unchanged. This supersedes the M5.a text 'sint_to_fp from I32/I64 conv.r4/conv.r8' for i64 to f32, and qualifies D17's 'conv.r4/conv.r8 of a non-negative int64 round once on every runtime' and recon/cil-runtime.md 6 ('int64->f32 is single-rounded on every runtime'): both hold only when the JIT does not fold the conversion. M5.c updates the sitofp_i64_f32 checks of fp-conv.ll (CIL and MIR) and rechecks the f32 part of u64-to-fp.ll | M5 gate-1 step 8: msil/test/M1/02-fp/conversions.ll exits 2 on .NET Framework 4.8 at -O0 and -O2 because sitofp_i64_f32(+/-(2^60+2^36+1)) returns +/-2^60 instead of +/-(2^60+2^37) once the JIT inlines the helper into a caller that passes the constant. Reproduced by triage with hand IL on Framework 4.8: conv.r4 of 1152921573326323713 gives float32 2^60 when inlined and 2^60+2^37 when not, and the D17 u64-to-f32 sequence marked aggressiveinlining gives 2^63 instead of 2^63+2^40 for 0x8000008000000001. With the step above, 18 inputs (both signs, around 2^53, 2^60, 2^62 and 2^63) are correct on Framework 4.8 inlined and not inlined, and on .NET 8 tiered and with TieredCompilation=0. The M5 exit criterion requires conversions to be correct on .NET Framework 4.8, and conversions.ll matches the spec |

| 2026-10-14 | M6 | M6.a extended (jump-table size): MSILTargetLowering overrides isSuitableForJumpTable so that a range of more than 4096 entries never becomes one jump table, at every optimization level and also in optsize and minsize functions (TargetLoweringBase::isSuitableForJumpTable skips getMaximumJumpTableSize() when optimizing for size), and otherwise defers to the base class. It does not call setMaximumJumpTableSize, which writes the process-wide `-max-jump-table-size` option shared by every target in the process (TargetLoweringBase.cpp:76 and 2274). LowerBR_JT asserts that the table fits in one SDNode (SDNode::getMaxNumOperands()). Switch lowering turns a larger range into several tables behind LLVM's range checks (D35). M6.c adds boundary cases to `switch.ll` at -O2 (no jump table is formed at -O0), with the IR generated at test time: a switch whose range is just above 4096 entries at the default 10% density, and one in an optsize function at 40% density, each compile with -verify-machineinstrs, print no 'switch' with more than 4096 targets, and assemble | M6 review-r1 finding 1 (verified with the M6 llc and clang): MSILISD::BR_TABLE carries each table entry as an SDNode operand (N + 3 operands), SDNode::NumOperands is 16 bits, and a table of 65533 or more entries asserts in 'MSIL Instruction Selection' (llc, and clang -O2 on a C switch of about 6,600 cases over a range above 65,532; a build without assertions would truncate the operand count and miscompile). 4096 stays far below the limit and keeps the boundary tests fast: with -verify-machineinstrs at -O2 a 4097-case switch takes 0.4 s, a 65533-case one over a minute (triage, mostly the quadratic cluster partitioning) |
| 2026-10-14 | M6 | Switch contract amended (sections 5.1 and 5.4, M6.a and M6.b): the last MBB operand of MSILISD::BR_TABLE and BR_TABLE_I32, and therefore the 'br' printed on the line after 'switch (...)', is the first jump-table entry, a dummy target that is already a CFG successor of the block (as in WebAssembly's LowerBR_JT), not the C switch's default block. It never executes: LLVM's range check, which is never removed (D35), sends every out-of-range value to the default before the 'switch', and when the default is unreachable an out-of-range index is undefined behaviour. M6.c pins this in its switch tests: the 'br' after each 'switch' names that switch's first label, and the last MBB operand of BR_TABLE_I32 in the MIR checks is the first one | M6 review-r1 finding 7 (verified): the implementation printed the first entry while 5.1, 5.4 and M6.b said 'default', and no test pinned either. The jump-table block cannot name the real default: BR_JT does not carry it, and an edge from the jump-table block to the default block would need PHI inputs there |

TableGen checks an instruction's flags against its patterns and rejects mayStore = 1 for `def : Pat<(atomic_fence (timm), (timm)), (MEMORY_BARRIER)>` ('Pattern doesn't match mayStore = 1'); mayLoad = 1 is accepted but adds nothing. The flags are not what keeps memory accesses on their side of the barrier: an instruction with unmodeled side effects and no memory operands has MachineInstr::hasOrderedMemoryRef() true, so MachineInstr::isSafeToMove and the schedulers treat it as a store, and MSILRegStackify's query() counts it as Write and Effects, as for the generic MEMBARRIER of a singlethread fence | M7 review-r2 finding 2 (verified with llvm-tblgen -gen-dag-isel and -gen-instr-info on a scratch copy of the MSIL .td files: mayLoad = 1 with mayStore = 1 fails with 'Pattern doesn't match mayStore = 1' and 'pattern conflicts', mayLoad = 1 alone passes); the record and its comment were right and the M7.a text was stale; no code or test change |

| 2026-10-14 | M8 | M8.c amended (alignment prefixes in `<Module>::.cctor`): the module initializer of recon/asmprinter.md 7.3 gets an `unaligned. N` line (printed on its own line, as MSILInstPrinter prints the memflags prefix) immediately before an `initblk` or a relocation `stind.i` / `stind.i4` whose address is known to be less aligned than the instruction's natural alignment as MSILSetMemFlags defines it (D24: the pointer size for `initblk` and `stind.i`, 4 for `stind.i4`). The address is Base + offset with Base aligned to MaxAlign, so its alignment is `commonAlignment(MaxAlign, offset)`, and N = min(that alignment, 4) (recon/isel.md section 7 table). CoreCLR x64 ignores the prefix, so this is conformance, not a Phase-1 failure. The cpblk sub-claim was not reproduced (its destination is Base, and Framework ilasm put the blob 16-aligned), so it is not changed (section 1.7) |

| 2026-10-14 | M8 | M8.a and M8.c amended (IL names of `<Module>` members): (1) a library call binds to the module function whose IL name (`MSIL::getILName`) is the symbol, found by the new `const Function *MSIL::getFunctionByILName(const Module &M, StringRef Name)` (MSILUtilities.h, M8.a: the function named Name, else the one named "\1" + Name, else null), which LowerCall and `MSILAsmPrinter::emitPInvokeDeclarations` use instead of `Module::getFunction`; (2) a used undefined function whose IL name and CIL signature equal those of a function defined in the module gets no P/Invoke declaration: its calls and `ldftn`s bind to that method, as a native link binds the symbol; (3) llc reports, with `LLVMContext::emitError` and exit code 1, what would otherwise print IL that ilasm rejects or the runtime cannot load: `global variable <IR> has the reserved IL name '<msil>data'`, `function <IR> has the reserved IL name '.cctor'` (likewise `'.ctor'`, for a function that gets a method or a P/Invoke declaration), `global variable <IR2> has the same IL name '<name>' as <IR1>` (two definitions, address fields of external globals included), `function <IR2> has the same IL name '<name>' and signature as <IR1>` (two definitions), and `function '<f>' refers to '<x>', which is not program data` (once per function and global, for code that reads a global that `MSIL::isSpecialLLVMGlobal` skips, such as one in section llvm.metadata; `<f>` and `<x>` are IL names). `<IR>` is `MSIL::getIRName`, and `<IR1>` is the earlier of the two in module order. Only the generated names that collide are reserved: any other `<msil>` name (identifier-quoting.ll's function `@"<msil>"`, a global named `<msil>image`, a function named `<msil>Main`, whose CIL signature C cannot produce) stays valid. | 2026-10-14 | - | Scope (user decision 1): like LLVM itself, the project ships compiler tools only (clang, llc, msil-ld, ...); there is no NuGet packaging (no packages, no nuspecs) and no MSBuild SDK, and build systems (CMake, MSBuild, ...) are separate projects that integrate the compiler. Changed: Design.md scope line (added); NuGet packaging and the MSBuild SDK removed from Design.md section 8 and the phase 5 row of section 11, and from section 7 P5-A, which keeps 32-bit triples and static host libcs | User decision |
| 2026-10-14 | - | Multi-target assembly (user decision 2): a single multi-target ("fat") assembly is no longer a non-goal; it is not planned for phases 1-5 and may be considered after them. Design.md no longer tells users to package several targets with NuGet's `runtimes/<rid>` layout: combining per-target assemblies is the user's build system's business. Changed: Design.md section 1 (the non-goal line removed) and 5.6 | User decision |
| 2026-10-14 | - | Runtime configuration files (user decision 3): like Roslyn (csc), which writes only the assembly (and PDB), the compiler driver and msil-ld do not write foo.runtimeconfig.json or foo.exe.config; those come from the build system or the user (the .NET SDK's MSBuild targets write them for C#). The test harness (msil-run.py, run scripts) keeps writing its own runtimeconfig for test runs; that is test tooling, not the compiler. Changed: Design.md section 8; section 7 P2-B; M10.d's GettingStarted wording and its Status.md item on 'dotnet prog.dll' (documentation only, no M8-M10 code or test changes) | User decision |
| 2026-10-14 | - | Assembly references (user decision 4) work like Roslyn's: no core-library switch and no target-framework option. Phase 1 keeps hard-coding [mscorlib] (D10 stands for Phase 1; .NET's mscorlib facade makes it run everywhere). From P2-B on, msil-ld is given reference assemblies (the option's spelling is left to P2-B: in lld, which msil-ld is modeled on, -r means --relocatable) and binds every BCL type and member the generated code needs (Marshal::AllocHGlobal, Interlocked::MemoryBarrier, Math, attributes such as DisableRuntimeMarshalling, ...) by searching those references, emitting whichever assembly defines it: .NET 8 reference assemblies yield System.Runtime/System.Runtime.InteropServices references, .NET Framework's yield mscorlib. In the .NET 8 reference pack Interlocked is defined in System.Threading (recon isel.md 5.9; re-checked in Microsoft.NETCore.App.Ref 8.0.31), so the edited passages name System.Threading as well. Changed: D10 (last sentence), section 7 P2-B, section 10 item 3 (resolution); Design.md 2.3, 5.2, 7.1 and the phase 2 row of section 11 | User decision |
| 2026-10-14 | - | Runtime targeting (user decision 5): the `-mdotnet=net48\|net8.0` profile is replaced by individual, independent codegen switches that permit specific newer APIs or attributes (for example "may use Math.FusedMultiplyAdd", "emit DisableRuntimeMarshalling", "use UnmanagedCallersOnly thunks instead of delegate thunks"), plus capability taken from the references where that is possible (an attribute or API is usable only if some reference defines it). A build system may combine switches into "net8.0" if it wants; the compiler has no profile concept. Design.md 7.1's account of what differs between .NET Framework 4.8 and modern .NET is kept: it decides which switches exist and their safe defaults, and the defaults keep working on .NET Framework 4.8 and modern .NET alike, as Phase 1 output does. The 7.1 table gains a floating-point math row (recon isel.md 3.2 and risk 8: Math.FusedMultiplyAdd, Math.CopySign, MathF and BitConverter.SingleToInt32Bits are missing on Framework, and Framework's Math.Min(-0.0, 0.0) is +0, a changed behaviour that the references cannot reveal, D16). Changed: D16 (last sentence), section 7 P3-A (title and text) and P3-B, section 10 item 3 (resolution), M10.d's Status.md list ('net48 profile flag'); Design.md 3.2, 4.3 (cross-reference to 7.1), 4.5, 5.3, section 7 (title, 7.1 text and table, 7.2), section 10 risk 4 and the phase 3 row of section 11 | User decision |
| 2026-10-15 | - | No runtime support assembly (user decision 6): MsilC.Runtime.dll (C#, net48 + net8.0) is dropped from the design and the plan. Generated code calls .NET (BCL) APIs directly wherever one exists: a [ThreadStatic] field plus NativeMemory or Marshal.AllocHGlobal for the per-thread shadow stack and the thread_local block, AppDomain.ProcessExit for global destructors and atexit, Environment.GetCommandLineArgs for argv, Interlocked/Volatile for atomics, System.Half for f16 and Int128/UInt128 for i128 division where available. Newer APIs are used only as decisions 4 and 5 allow (individual codegen switches, capability from the reference assemblies msil-ld binds against, defaults that run on .NET Framework 4.8 and modern .NET alike). Where no suitable BCL API exists, or the default must also run on .NET Framework 4.8, the backend or msil-ld emits small helpers into the output assembly itself, private to it: IL generated by the toolchain, or C helpers compiled by this toolchain and linked statically by msil-ld, the way LLVM links compiler-rt builtins (for example C complex __muldc3/__divdc3, whose Annex G infinity and NaN rules System.Numerics.Complex does not follow). setjmp/longjmp throws a BCL exception type and catches it with an IL exception filter that matches the jmp_buf, so no exception type has to be shared between assemblies. Process startup is the generated '<msil>Main' that Phase 1 already emits (M3, D27). Every output assembly is self-contained: it needs no runtime support assembly, and its only dependencies are the BCL, the native libraries it P/Invokes, and any assemblies it was explicitly linked against (P2-C). Not decided: whether libm calls move from P/Invoke to the host libm to Math/MathF; they stay as designed, a host-libc import (D14; Design.md 6). Math.Round's default rounding, fmod versus IEEERemainder and errno block a blanket mapping. Changed: section 7 P3-A (title and text) and P3-B; Design.md 4.6, section 6 (the runtime support assembly bullet, and 'written in C#' dropped from the deferred portable libc's OS layer), the 7.1 table (128-bit integers row, new half-precision row), section 8 (driver bullet) and the phase 3 row of section 11. No M0-M10 text changes (the 'phase 3' diagnostic texts of M7 and M8 stay accurate) | User decision |

| 2026-10-15 | M9 | Phase-1 function addresses (amends plan 5.1 'Calls' (`ldftn int32 'f'(int32)`), the 5.4 catalogue entry `FUNC_ADDR` (ldftn), the M8.a and M8.c texts 'FUNC_ADDR prints ldftn <member-ref>' and '.cctor ... function relocations via ldftn <sig>', and the M9 goal and M9.c wording 'ldftn identity'): every function whose address is used, by FUNC_ADDR in code or as the target of a data-segment relocation (a definition, or an undefined function, whose ldftn names its P/Invoke declaration; an undefined function that is diagnosed keeps its field and its .cctor pair, as referenced extern globals keep their fields; a constructor that the .cctor only calls is not "used"), gets one address field `.field private static initonly native int '<msil>fn_<IL name>'` (MSILTargetStreamer::emitAddressField with Generated=true; IL name = MSIL::getILName). The fields are declared after the data-segment block (after the address fields of the global variables and of referenced extern globals and their empty line) and before the .cctor, one line per function in module order (`Module::functions()`), then one empty line, with no header comment. `<Module>::.cctor` sets each field once, in the same order, with `ldftn <member-ref>` then `stsfld native int '<msil>fn_<IL name>'`, right after the stsfld of every global's address field and before the relocations, or first when the module has no data segment; the .cctor is emitted whenever the module has such a field, even with no data and no constructors. A relocation to a function stores `ldsfld native int '<msil>fn_<IL name>'` (then conv.i4 + stind.i4 or stind.i as before) instead of `ldftn`, and FUNC_ADDR in code prints `ldsfld native int '<msil>fn_<IL name>'`, so `ldftn` appears only in the .cctor, once per function. FUNC_ADDR keeps its operand list, operand type, stack effect and rematerialization; only its text (and CIL encoding) changes. Calls through a pointer stay managed `calli` (Design.md 12 'ldftn + calli'); the field is the one per-module slot per address-taken function that Design.md 4.5's thunk table fills later. A global variable whose address field would have the name of a function address field, and two used functions with the same IL name, are errors. | M9 gate-1 step 7: fnptr-call.c:178-179 failed on .NET Framework 4.8 at -O0 and -O2. On Framework, `ldftn` in a method that the JIT compiles before the target first ran yields the target's precode, and in one compiled after it the native code (triage probe `ident.il`: two methods with `ldftn int32 'add1'(int32)` returned 140708193436808 and 140708193438112; two methods reading one field set by ldftn in the .cctor returned the same value, and calli through it ran), so C function-pointer equality (C11 6.5.9p6) and the exit criterion 'Function-pointer dispatch ... on both triples and all runtimes' failed; recon cil-runtime.md e07 checked identity only within one JIT state. A test of C semantics is not weakened (section 1.7); the .NET 8/10 behaviour is unchanged |

| 2026-10-15 | M9 | M9.b amended (prototype-less functions whose address is used): MSILAddMissingPrototypes derives the signature of a "no-prototype" declaration only from its direct calls (the call type, made non-variadic). A declaration that has no direct call when the pass runs but whose address is used (stored, passed, compared or in a global initializer; dead constant users do not count) is an error, `the signature of prototype-less function '<name>' cannot be derived: its address is used, but it is never called directly; declare it with a prototype` (LLVMContext::emitError, a module-level diagnostic without a source location, like the pass's other "no-prototype" diagnostics), instead of WebAssembly's fallback signature `T ()`, because a managed calli does not check signatures and a P/Invoke declared `T ()` would receive garbage arguments. The pass runs after the IR optimizer, so whether valid C hits the error depends on the optimization level: at -O1 and above the middle end (IPSCCP, inlining) may turn the call through the pointer into a direct call, from which the signature is derived, so the same source can fail at -O0 and compile correctly at -O2. Only undefined (imported) functions get "no-prototype", so this affects a prototype-less import whose address is taken; it is a documented limitation, not a miscompile, and C sources (the M1 tests and the M10 corpus, kr-legacy included) declare such an import with a prototype (the C headers and msil_test.h do). clang/test/CodeGen/MSIL/no-proto-address.c pins both levels | M9 review-r2 finding 4 (verified, scratch verify-knrptr): 'int puts();' whose address is passed to a noinline 'call(int (*f)(const char *), const char *)' fails at -O0 on both x86_64 MSIL triples and compiles and runs at -O1, -O2 and -Os (a direct `call int32 'puts'(native int)`); a volatile pointer fails at both levels. The error came from the review-r1 fix and was recorded nowhere, while plan 5.5 asks tests to behave identically at -O0 and -O2 |

No C-level test covered it (only add-prototypes.ll's IR part addr-only.ll), and no M9 WP owns clang/test/CodeGen/MSIL |

| 2026-10-20 | - | Upstream merges (branch model row of 2026-10-13) also maintain msil/utils/upstream-hooks.txt: every upstream file the fork changes (git diff against the merged upstream commit, outside the fork-only directories) has a hook block naming its upstream lit tests, unit tests, compile coverage or the reason it has none, and fork files inside upstream directories are listed as fork-only. The merge gate runs msil/utils/ci/upstream-hooks.ps1 with every leg (manifest, pins, lit, gtest, compile) on the uncommitted merge, so an unlisted or stale hook, a new exhaustive object-format switch without an MSIL case (qualified or not), a failing upstream test of a hooked file or an action pin that drifted from upstream's fails the merge. The manifest leg's base is `MSIL_UPSTREAM_BASE` if set, else MERGE_HEAD while a merge is in progress (the new upstream commit), else the second parent of the last first-parent commit whose subject starts '[MSIL] Merge upstream llvm-project main at ' (merge commits keep that subject). The allow-list takes only per-test environment failures that name the missing tool. This curated check runs on every gate and CI run; lit-legs.sh's `--full` 'upstream' leg (`ninja check-llvm check-clang`) stays the full upstream regression run for nightly Linux CI | Items (2) and (7) of the deferred row: the merge review found these gaps and its evidence was lost, so the manifest and the legs are the durable record. (b) switch-default and switch-other are checked per switch, not per file: in a switch-default file each XCOFF case label must lie in a switch whose own body (not a nested switch) has a `default:` label or an MSIL case label, else the label's line is reported; a switch-other line exempts only the unqualified `case XCOFF:` and `case IsXCOFF:` labels of its file, and qualified `Triple::XCOFF` and `MCContext::IsXCOFF` labels there are checked as in any other file; a switch-default line whose file has no XCOFF switch, or a switch-other line whose file has no unqualified XCOFF label, is stale. ## Appendix A. Review dispositions (plan v1 -> v2)

Three reviewers (feasibility, agent-farm protocol, CIL fidelity) found 48 issues; several are the same problem seen
through different lenses. Each was checked against the source before it was accepted. "Accepted" means the fix is in
this plan, and the last column says where.

| # | Lens | Sev. | Ms | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | feasibility | blocker | M3 | -O0 smoke tests need frames (`main` gets a return-value alloca on windows-msvc) | Accepted. Re-verified with VS clang 22.1.3: windows-msvc -O0 gives `%1 = alloca i32` / `store i32 0`; linux-gnu -O0 gives only `ret i32 42`. M3 goal and exit criteria now say clang -O2, llc -O0/-O2; M3.e uses `%{msil_c_O2}`; M7.d owns `00-smoke/return-*.c` and switches them; D32 generalized to every C test before M7. |
| F2 | feasibility | blocker | M5 | D17's 'return SDValue()' needs BITCAST via memory (f64) and SELECT (f32); fabs/copysign go through memory too | Accepted, option (c) generalized to both types: D17 is now the select-free, memory-free sequence (verified `TargetLowering.cpp:10379-10380`, `LegalizeDAG.cpp:2884-2934`). fabs/copysign/bitcast tests move to M7.c and M7.e; plan 5.5 forbids them in 02-fp. |
| F3 | feasibility | blocker | M4 | Variable i128 shifts need SELECT | Accepted by moving SELECT to M4 (D34), so i128 variable shifts stay in M4 (verified `TargetLowering.cpp:9452-9457`). |
| F4 | feasibility | blocker | M6 | -O2 switches become `@switch.table` globals + loads | Accepted (re-verified: VS clang -O2 builds `@switch.table.pick` plus GEP and load even with -ffreestanding). `shouldBuildLookupTables()` false in M2.a's TTI, which is final in M2 (verified `BasicTTIImpl.h:644-648`); M6.d's switch.c also avoids constant-only arms; tti-hooks.ll tests it in M2. |
| F5 | feasibility | major | M4 | Generic code emits SELECT from M4 on (sext(setcc), min/max, shifts) | Accepted (D34): SELECT pseudos and emitSelect in M4.b; `convertSelectOfConstantsToMath` true, final in M2.f as a query hook (verified `DAGCombiner.cpp:16088-16098`, `TargetLowering.h:2634`). |
| F6 | feasibility | major | M7 | Initialized local aggregates and string literals become `@__const` globals even at -O0 | Accepted (re-verified: -O0 gives `@__const.sum.a` (align 16), `@__const.sum.s`, `@__const.sum.t` and three `llvm.memcpy`). Capability table in 5.5 and the M7.d/M7.e initializer rule; `07-globals/local-aggregate-init.c` added in M8.e. |
| F7 | feasibility | major | M4 | MSIL_EXPECT needs branches | Accepted: branch-free `MSIL_CHECK` in msil_test.h (M0.b), required before M6 (5.5, D37, M4.e). |
| F8 | feasibility | major | M2 | isLegalAddressingMode is a fatal stub reachable from TTI at -O2 | Accepted: final `[reg]`-only body in M2.f (verified `TargetTransformInfoImpl.h:1423`); rule that query hooks are never stubs (section 2, D33); tti-hooks.ll runs `opt -O2` on GEP code in M2. |
| F9 | feasibility | major | M5 | update_llc_test_checks gate is always UNSUPPORTED on Windows | Accepted (verified `llvm/test/tools/UpdateTestChecks/lit.local.cfg` probes `llc` without `.exe`): M5.b adds run-utc-tests.py and the gate runs it on msil-basic and x86-basic. Rejected part: 'fail the gate if lit reports UNSUPPORTED', because without changing that upstream file (which the same finding forbids) it is always UNSUPPORTED here. |
| F10 | feasibility | minor | M2 | MinGW triples get `-exception-model=seh` and the TM rejects it | Accepted (verified `MinGW.cpp:645-650`, `BackendUtil.cpp:388-389`): M2.a forces ExceptionModel None; tested in M2.e empty-tu.c and M3.e emit-il.c, earlier than the suggested M10.e. |

| F12 | feasibility | minor | M1 | Syntax checks impossible before any TableGen output exists | Accepted: M1.0 build-agent pre-build (targets verified in build.ninja), plus a re-run after M1.c. |

Module asm moved to `AsmPrinter::doInitialization`, because the legacy pass manager initializes the AsmPrinter before any IR pass runs (`LegacyPassManager.cpp` MPPassManager::runOnModule). |
